3. Take reciprocals (reversing inequalities): cos(x) < sin(x)/x < 1.  
4. As x → 0, cos(x) → 1, so sin(x)/x is squeezed between 1 and 1.  
5. By the squeeze theorem, sin(x)/x → 1.  

The final answer is **1**.

Final answer: (x/2)√(4 − x²) + 2 arcsin(x/2) + C.

Output:  The problem asks for the sum of 1/n² from n=1 to infinity.  
1. Expand f(x) = x² on [-π, π] as a Fourier series: x² = π²/3 + 4Σ [(-1)^n / n² cos(nx)].  
2. Plug x = π into the series: π² = π²/3 + 4Σ [(-1)^n / n² * (-1)^n].  
3. Simplify: π² = π²/3 + 4Σ [1/n²].  
4. Solve for Σ [1/n²]: π² - π²/3 = 4Σ [1/n²] ⇒ 2π²/3 = 4Σ [1/n²].  
5. Final result: Σ [1/n²] = π²/6.  

**Answer:** The sum is π²⁄6.
